A little, but I think it’s mostly when that work situation is hanging over me. I wouldn’t say I’m just down all the time or unable to enjoy anything… I don’t want to overstate it.

The district put this bilingual social-emotional learning rollout on my plate, on top of my regular teaching, and I’m still getting through the school day—I don’t want to make it sound worse than it is—but I feel kind of wound up and discouraged in a way that doesn’t really feel like me.\n\nAnd I’ve noticed I’m putting off lesson plans and emails that I normally would just handle without thinking. I’m not sure if that’s the sort of thing you mean, but it’s been bothering me enough that I thought I should talk to someone.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed that you’re not enjoying things that you usually find pleasurable or interesting lately?\n\nPatient: I’m not sure I’d say I’ve lost interest, exactly. I still like the things I usually like… I think. Maybe I’m a little less eager to do them when I’m tired, but I wouldn’t want to overstate it. Is that the kind of thing you’re asking?\n\nDoctor: Have you found that you don’t take as much pleasure in things you used to enjoy, even small things, not just when you’re tired, but more consistently?\n\nPatient: I don’t think so, not in a steady way. The district put this bilingual social-emotional learning rollout on my plate, on top of my regular teaching, and I’m still getting through the school day—I don’t want to make it sound worse than it is—but I feel kind of wound up and discouraged in a way that doesn’t really feel like me.\n\nAnd I’ve noticed I’m putting off lesson plans and emails that I normally would just handle without thinking. I’m not sure if that’s the sort of thing you mean, but it’s been bothering me enough that I thought I should talk to someone.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed that you’re not enjoying things that you usually find pleasurable or interesting lately?\n\nPatient: I’m not sure I’d say I’ve lost interest, exactly. I still like the things I usually like… I think. Maybe I’m a little less eager to do them when I’m tired, but I wouldn’t want to overstate it. Is that the kind of thing you’re asking?\n\nDoctor: Have you found that you don’t take as much pleasure in things you used to enjoy, even small things, not just when you’re tired, but more consistently?\n\nPatient: I don’t think so, not in a steady way. I mean, I still enjoy little things when I actually get to them—like talking with a friend or having a quiet moment. I might be less enthusiastic lately, but it feels more like I’m preoccupied or worn down, not that the pleasure is gone. Does that make sense?\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed feeling more fatigued or as if you have less energy lately, even when you haven’t been doing anything particularly taxing?\n\nPatient: I don’t think I’ve really noticed that, no. I mean, I get tired like anyone does after a school day, but not in a way where I feel drained for no reason. It’s more… normal tired, I guess. If that’s helpful?\n\nDoctor: Have you had any trouble falling asleep, staying asleep, or waking up earlier than planned, even when you’re not particularly stressed about anything?\n\nPatient: Not really, no. I mean, every now and then I might take a little while to settle down, but I think that’s pretty normal. I haven’t noticed a pattern of waking up too early or being up half the night or anything like that. Is that what you mean?\n\nDoctor: Have you found yourself feeling more anxious or worried than usual about things, even when there isn’t a clear reason or when the worry feels out of proportion to the situation?\n\nPatient: A little, yes… though I don’t want to make it sound worse than it is. Since the district put this bilingual social-emotional learning rollout on me, on top of my regular teaching, I’ve felt more wound up and discouraged than I’m used to. I’m still getting through the day, but I notice I’m putting off lesson plans and emails that I normally would just handle. So I guess the worry is there, but it feels tied to that situation, if that makes sense.\n\nDoctor: When you say the worry feels tied to the situation with the rollout, does that mean the anxiety stops once you're not thinking about work, or does it linger even when you're away from those responsibilities?\n\nPatient: It doesn’t completely disappear when I’m away from work, but it’s definitely connected to that rollout. I can be home and not actively working, and then I’ll remember the lesson plans or emails I’ve been putting off, and I get that wound-up feeling again.\n\nI mean, I’m still functioning, I’m still getting through the school day and all of that. It’s just… since this got added on top of my regular teaching, I’ve felt more discouraged than I usually do, and I don’t like that I’m avoiding things I normally would have just handled. Does that make sense?\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed feeling down or having trouble enjoying things even during times when work or the rollout isn’t on your mind?\n\nPatient: A little, but I think it’s mostly when that work situation is hanging over me. I wouldn’t say I’m just down all the time or unable to enjoy anything… I don’t want to overstate it.\n\nIt’s more that since the district put this bilingual social-emotional learning rollout on my plate, on top of my regular teaching, I feel wound up and discouraged in a way that isn’t really like me. I’m still getting through the day, but I’ve started putting off lesson plans and emails I used to just handle without thinking. So yes, I guess there’s some feeling down, but it feels tied to all of that.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed feeling anxious or on edge even when you're not thinking about work or the rollout, like a general sense of worry that isn't tied to any specific thing?\n\nPatient: Not really as a general thing, I don’t think. I mean, I don’t want to say I’m perfectly calm all the time, but it doesn’t feel like worry out of nowhere.\n\nIt’s more that ever since the district put that bilingual social-emotional learning rollout on my plate, on top of my regular teaching, I’ve felt kind of wound up and discouraged in a way that isn’t really like me. I’m still getting through the day, but I’ve started putting off lesson plans and emails I used to just handle without a second thought. I’m still functioning and getting through the day, but I’ve been putting off lesson plans and emails that I normally would’ve just handled. So the down feeling is there, but it really seems connected to all of that.\n\nDoctor: Looking back, have you had times when even things you usually enjoy didn’t feel as rewarding or satisfying as they normally do, even when you weren’t thinking about work?\n\nPatient: I don’t think so, not really. I mean, I still enjoy things when I’m actually doing them. Maybe I’m a little more distracted sometimes, but I wouldn’t say things have stopped feeling rewarding or satisfying.\n\nI hope that makes sense… it doesn’t feel like I’ve lost interest across the board. It’s more just that the work-related stuff can hang over me a bit.\n\nDoctor: Looking back, have you found yourself worrying about multiple things — not just work — in a way that feels excessive or hard to control, even when those things weren’t actually that likely to happen?\n\nPatient: I don’t think it’s multiple things, no. If anything, it’s that one area that’s been taking up more space than I’d like. Is that answering what you mean?\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any persistent feelings of anxiety or worry that don’t seem to be related to the rollout at work, something more general that lingers even when you’re not thinking about your job?\n\nPatient: I don’t think I’ve noticed that, no. I’m still getting through the day, but I notice I’m putting off lesson plans and emails that I normally would just handle. I can be home and not actively working, and then I’ll remember the lesson plans or emails I’ve been putting off, and I get that wound-up feeling again.\n\nI mean, I’m still functioning, I’m still getting through the school day and all of that. It’s just… since this got added on top of my regular teaching, I’ve felt more discouraged than I usually do, and I don’t like that I’m avoiding things I normally would have just handled. Does that make sense?\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed feeling down or having trouble enjoying things even during times when work or the rollout isn’t on your mind?\n\nPatient: A little, but I think it’s mostly when that work situation is hanging over me. I wouldn’t say I’m just down all the time or unable to enjoy anything… I don’t want to overstate it.\n\nIt’s more that since the district put this bilingual social-emotional learning rollout on my plate, on top of my regular teaching, I feel wound up and discouraged in a way that isn’t really like me. I’m still getting through the day, but I’ve started putting off lesson plans and emails I used to just handle without thinking. Is that the kind of thing you’re asking about?\n\nDoctor: Looking back over the past few weeks, have you had days where you felt down or hopeless most of the time, even when you weren’t thinking about work or the rollout?\n\nPatient: I don’t think I’d say hopeless most of the time, no. It’s more just that the work-related stuff can hang over me a bit.\n\nDoctor: Looking back, have you found yourself worrying about multiple things — not just work — in a way that feels excessive or hard to control, even when those things weren’t actually that likely to happen?\n\nPatient: I don’t think it’s multiple things, no.
